`CMakeLists.txt`:

```txt
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.20)
project(VMProtectResourceExtractor LANGUAGES CXX)
add_executable(vmp-resource-extractor standalone/main.cpp)
target_compile_features(vmp-resource-extractor PRIVATE cxx_std_17)
if(MSVC)
  target_compile_options(vmp-resource-extractor PRIVATE /W4 /utf-8)
  set_property(TARGET vmp-resource-extractor PROPERTY MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY "MultiThreaded$<$<CONFIG:Debug>:Debug>")
endif()

```

`README.md`:

```md
# VMProtect Resource Extractor

I wrote this after tracing VMProtect's Resource Protection across versions 3.3.1 to 3.10.1. It finds the encrypted resource table, recovers its key and extracts the resources from EXE and DLL files.

There is a standalone extractor, a Unicorn-based emulator for packed files, and an x64dbg plugin. None of them needs the original unprotected file or a hardcoded address.

![VMProtect Resource Extractor](docs/images/plugin.png)

## Download

**[v1.0.0](https://github.com/ivanpotatoes/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor/releases/tag/v1.0.0)**

- [Static extractor](https://github.com/ivanpotatoes/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor/releases/download/v1.0.0/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor-v1.0.0-static-x64.zip)
- [Emulator](https://github.com/ivanpotatoes/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor/releases/download/v1.0.0/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor-v1.0.0-emulation-x64.zip)
- [x64dbg plugin](https://github.com/ivanpotatoes/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor/releases/download/v1.0.0/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor-v1.0.0-x64dbg-x64.zip)

These are Windows x64 builds. No kernel driver or separate Python installation is needed. Checksums, licenses and dependency sources are included in the release.

## Standalone

Start with the static extractor if the resource table and encrypted data are already in the file:

```text
vmp-resource-extractor.exe protected.exe
vmp-resource-extractor.exe protected.dll --output extracted
```

By default, the output goes into `protected.exe.resources` beside the input. Use a new folder for each extraction; existing folders are left alone.

For a packed file, try the emulator:

```text
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.exe --output extracted
```

It runs the protected startup code in Unicorn until the resources become available. It does not launch the file as a Windows process. Python, Unicorn and Capstone are bundled in the executable.

The default limits are 240 seconds and five billion instructions. A larger file may need more:

```text
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.dll --seconds 600 --instructions 15000000000
```

The emulator handles the startup paths I tested. An unsupported API or exception can still stop it. The three packed test files, timings and implementation details are in [the emulation notes](docs/EMULATION.md).

## x64dbg

1. Copy `VMPResourceExtractor.dp64` into the `plugins` folder beside `x64dbg.exe` and start the debugger.
2. Open **Plugins > VMP Resource Extractor > Extract resources...**

![Plugin menu](docs/images/menu.png)

3. Load your program and let it finish unpacking. For a DLL, wait until the host has loaded and initialized it. Select the module in the plugin and click **Scan**.
4. Choose a new output folder and click **Extract**. If more than one table was found, select one first.

**Scan pauses a running target and leaves it paused.** Press F9 when you want to continue. If you resume execution or a DLL is loaded or unloaded, scan again before extracting.

Use **Refresh** to update the module list. **Browse** picks a parent directory and suggests a new output folder. The plugin reads memory without changing your code or breakpoint settings.

The same actions are available from the command bar:

```text
vre_ui
vre_scan
vre_extract "C:\work\extracted"
vre_dump "C:\work\extracted-next"
vre_status
```

`vre_dump` scans and extracts in one command. See [plugin commands and capture limits](docs/PLUGIN.md) for module selection and scripting.

## Output

You get `resource-0001.bin`, `resource-0002.bin`, and so on, plus `manifest.json`. The manifest records each resource's type, name or ID, language, codepage, size and RVA.

The files contain the raw resource bytes. A dialog stays a binary dialog resource, and an icon resource is not automatically converted to an ICO file. The input file is unchanged.

This is an extractor. It does not rebuild `.rsrc` or produce an unpacked executable. Ordinary resources still present in the PE resource directory, such as its manifest, are outside the encrypted tree and are not exported.

## Tested versions

| VMProtect | Static x64 EXE and DLL | Live x64dbg extraction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3.3.1 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.4.0 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.5.0 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.6.0 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.8.0 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.9.4 | Verified | Verified |
| 3.10.1 | Verified | Verified |

The static and mapped-image tests recovered **126 resources from 21 files**, with every byte matching the originals. These files had only Resource Protection enabled. They cover menus, dialogs, string tables, custom types, named resources and multiple languages.

Live extraction has also been checked on each version above, including my tests of 3.4.0, 3.5.0, 3.6.0, 3.8.0 and 3.9.4. Packed-file emulation was tested separately on the three files listed in the emulation notes.

Real protected x32 files have not been tested. The emulator and plugin currently support x64 only.

## Build

Install Visual Studio C++ Build Tools and the Windows SDK, then run:

```powershell
.\build.cmd
.\build-plugin.cmd "C:\path\to\pluginsdk"
```

The binaries go into `dist`. [Build instructions](docs/BUILDING.md) cover the emulator, dependencies and tests.

```md
# Bundled components

The emulation executable bundles these unmodified libraries and the Python runtime:

- Unicorn Engine 2.1.4 — GPL-2.0, with additional component notices. Source: https://github.com/unicorn-engine/unicorn/tree/2.1.4
- Capstone 5.0.9 — BSD license. Source: https://github.com/capstone-engine/capstone
- pefile 2024.8.26 — MIT license. Source: https://github.com/erocarrera/pefile
- CPython 3.14.0 — Python Software Foundation license. Source: https://github.com/python/cpython/tree/v3.14.0
- PyInstaller 6.22.3 bootloader — GPL with its distribution exception. Source: https://github.com/pyinstaller/pyinstaller/tree/v6.22.3

License texts are in `licenses`. The PyInstaller bootloader is compiled from the upstream source with `--no-cfg` for compatibility with Unicorn. Its source is not modified; the local build commands are in `build-bootloader.cmd` and `build-emulation.cmd`.

The [v1.0.0 release](https://github.com/ivanpotatoes/VMProtect-Resource-Extractor/releases/tag/v1.0.0) also provides `VMProtect-Resource-Extractor-v1.0.0-emulation-sources.zip`, containing the matching Unicorn 2.1.4 and PyInstaller 6.22.3 source archives. SHA-256 values are included. Project source and build scripts are available from the same release tag.

```

`build-block-budget.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
for /f "delims=" %%I in ('py -c "import capstone,pathlib; print(pathlib.Path(capstone.__file__).parent / 'include')"') do set "CAPSTONE_INCLUDE=%%I"
if not exist build mkdir build
if not exist dist mkdir dist
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /utf-8 /LD /I"%CAPSTONE_INCLUDE%" standalone\block_budget.cpp /Fo:build\block_budget.obj /Fe:dist\block_budget.dll /link /INCREMENTAL:NO /IMPLIB:build\block_budget.lib
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-bootloader.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
if not exist build\vendor\pyinstaller-6.22.3\bootloader\waf (
  py -m pip download pyinstaller==6.22.3 --no-binary=pyinstaller --no-deps --dest build\vendor
  if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
  py -c "import tarfile; tarfile.open('build/vendor/pyinstaller-6.22.3.tar.gz').extractall('build/vendor',filter='data')"
  if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
)
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
cd build\vendor\pyinstaller-6.22.3\bootloader
py waf all --no-cfg
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-emulation.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
call build.cmd
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
call build-native-tracker.cmd
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
call build-native-scanner.cmd
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
call build-block-budget.cmd
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
if not exist build\vendor\pyinstaller-6.22.3\PyInstaller\bootloader\Windows-64bit-intel\run.exe call build-bootloader.cmd
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
set "PYTHONPATH=%CD%\build\vendor\pyinstaller-6.22.3"
py -m PyInstaller --noconfirm --clean --onefile --name vmp-resource-emulator --distpath dist --workpath build\pyinstaller --specpath build --collect-all unicorn --collect-all capstone --add-binary "%CD%\dist\vmp-resource-extractor.exe;." --add-binary "%CD%\dist\vmp-native-tracker.dll;." --add-binary "%CD%\dist\resource_scanner.dll;." --add-binary "%CD%\dist\block_budget.dll;." standalone\emulate.py
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-native-scanner.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
cd /d "%~dp0"
if not exist build mkdir build
if not exist dist mkdir dist
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /utf-8 /LD standalone\native_scanner.cpp /Fo:build\native_scanner.obj /Fe:dist\resource_scanner.dll /link /INCREMENTAL:NO /IMPLIB:build\resource_scanner.lib
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-native-tracker.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
cd /d "%~dp0"
if not exist build mkdir build
if not exist dist mkdir dist
py -c "from pathlib import Path; import unicorn; print(Path(unicorn.__file__).parent / 'include')" > build\unicorn-include.txt
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
set /p "UNICORN_HEADERS=" < build\unicorn-include.txt
if not exist "%UNICORN_HEADERS%\unicorn\unicorn.h" (
  echo Unicorn headers not found: %UNICORN_HEADERS%
  exit /b 1
)
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /WX /LD /utf-8 /I"%UNICORN_HEADERS%" standalone\native_tracker.cpp /Fo:build\native_tracker.obj /Fe:dist\vmp-native-tracker.dll /link /INCREMENTAL:NO /IMPLIB:build\native_tracker.lib
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-plugin-tests.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
if not exist build\plugin-tests mkdir build\plugin-tests
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /WX /utf-8 plugin\backend.cpp tests\plugin_backend_harness.cpp /Fo:build\plugin-tests\ /Fe:build\plugin-backend-tests.exe
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build-plugin.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
if not "%~1"=="" set "VRE_SDK=%~1"
if not defined VRE_SDK (
  echo Pass the x64dbg pluginsdk path as the first argument or set VRE_SDK.
  exit /b 1
)
if not exist "%VRE_SDK%\_plugins.h" (
  echo The selected SDK has no _plugins.h. Pass the pluginsdk path or set VRE_SDK.
  exit /b 1
)
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
if not exist build\plugin mkdir build\plugin
if not exist dist mkdir dist
rc /nologo /i plugin /fo build\plugin\dialog.res plugin\dialog.rc
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /utf-8 /LD /DUNICODE /D_UNICODE /DWIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN /I"%VRE_SDK%" /Iplugin plugin\plugin.cpp plugin\ui.cpp plugin\backend.cpp build\plugin\dialog.res /Fo:build\plugin\ /Fe:dist\VMPResourceExtractor.dp64 /link /INCREMENTAL:NO /IMPLIB:build\plugin\VMPResourceExtractor.lib "%VRE_SDK%\x64dbg.lib" "%VRE_SDK%\x64bridge.lib" comctl32.lib shell32.lib ole32.lib uuid.lib user32.lib gdi32.lib
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`build.cmd`:

```cmd
@echo off
setlocal
call "%~dp0scripts\find-msvc.cmd"
if errorlevel 1 exit /b 1
cd /d "%~dp0"
if not exist build mkdir build
if not exist dist mkdir dist
cl /nologo /std:c++17 /EHsc /O2 /MT /W4 /utf-8 standalone\main.cpp /Fo:build\extractor.obj /Fe:dist\vmp-resource-extractor.exe /link /INCREMENTAL:NO
exit /b %errorlevel%

```

`docs/BUILDING.md`:

```md
# Building and testing

Run commands from the repository root. The build scripts target Windows x64 and use Visual Studio's C++ toolchain with the Windows SDK. They reuse an existing x64 developer shell or find an installed toolchain through `vswhere`.

## Static extractor

```powershell
.\build.cmd
```

Output: `dist/vmp-resource-extractor.exe`. The MSVC runtime is statically linked; the executable does not require Python.

Alternatively, build the static extractor with CMake:

```text
cmake -S . -B build-cmake -A x64
cmake --build build-cmake --config Release
```

CMake writes its executable under the selected build directory. The Python tests expect the command-script output in `dist`.

## x64dbg plugin

Use the x64dbg plugin SDK that contains `_plugins.h`, `x64dbg.lib` and `x64bridge.lib`:

```powershell
.\build-plugin.cmd "C:\tools\x64dbg\pluginsdk"
```

Or set the SDK path for the current shell:

```powershell
$env:VRE_SDK = 'C:\tools\x64dbg\pluginsdk'
.\build-plugin.cmd
```

Output: `dist/VMPResourceExtractor.dp64`. Copy it into the `plugins` directory beside `x64dbg.exe`. The plugin does not need the standalone extractor or Python at runtime.

## Emulator

The packaged build was tested with 64-bit CPython 3.14.0. Install the pinned dependencies and PyInstaller:

```powershell
py -m pip install -r requirements-emulation.txt pyinstaller==6.22.3
.\build-emulation.cmd
```

The build script compiles the static extractor and three native helpers, then creates `dist/vmp-resource-emulator.exe`. Python and the required libraries are bundled in that executable.

The first build downloads the unmodified PyInstaller 6.22.3 source and compiles its bootloader with `--no-cfg`. This is a build setting for compatibility with Unicorn's context switching. The source and generated files stay under `build/vendor`. See [third-party notices](../THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md) for component versions and licenses.

To run directly from Python, build the helpers first:

```powershell
.\build.cmd
.\build-native-tracker.cmd
.\build-native-scanner.cmd
.\build-block-budget.cmd
py standalone/emulate.py protected.exe --output extracted
```

## Tests without research fixtures

With the static executable built and dependencies installed:

```powershell
py tests/verify_synthetic.py
py tests/verify_snapshot_io.py
```

After building the native helpers, run:

```powershell
py tests/test_native_tracker.py
py tests/test_native_support.py
py tests/test_host_image.py
py tests/test_block_budget.py
py tests/test_timeout_state.py
```

These cover generated PE layouts, Unicode names, ambiguous or malformed tables, memory tracking, host-image access, execution budgets and timeout handling.

## Research fixture tests

Protected research binaries are not included in this repository. The integration tests expect matching unprotected and protected files in this layout:

```text
resource_protection/
  originals/x64/
    ResourceSample-x64.exe
    ResourcePaths-x64.exe
    ResourceFixture-x64.dll
  protected/<version>/x64/
    ResourceSample-x64.exe
    ResourcePaths-x64.exe
    ResourceFixture-x64.dll
```

Version folders are `3.3.1`, `3.4.0`, `3.5.0`, `3.6.0`, `3.8.0`, `3.9.4` and `3.10.1`. These fixtures use Resource Protection only.

The default fixture root is `../samples/resource_protection`, relative to the repository. Set `VRE_SAMPLES` to use another location for the fixture tests and benchmarks:

```powershell
$env:VRE_SAMPLES = 'C:\research\resource_protection'
py tests/verify_samples.py
py tests/test_native_scanner.py
.\build-plugin-tests.cmd
py tests/test_plugin_backend.py
```

The plugin-backend harness uses the same capture and extraction code as the plugin, with mapped fixture images and a controlled memory reader. Live debugger extraction is checked separately by comparing its output with an original PE:

```text
py tests/verify_plugin_output.py original.exe extracted-folder
```

Packed-fixture tests and benchmarks also use `ResourcePaths-x64.vmp.exe`, `ResourceSample-x64.vmp.exe` and `pawdResourceFixture-x64.dll` under the fixture root's `originals/x64` directory:

```powershell
py tests/verify_emulation_models.py
py tests/benchmark_emulation.py --mode packaged --scheduler block
py tests/benchmark_emulation.py --mode packaged --reference
```

Each benchmark run creates a new directory under `build/optimization`, records budgets and timings, compares every resource against the original and checks that the input hash is unchanged. See [emulation details](EMULATION.md) for the measured results and what the execution budget means.

## Release packages

After building all three deliverables, package the existing binaries:

```powershell
py -m pip download --no-deps --no-binary=:all: --dest build/vendor unicorn==2.1.4 pyinstaller==6.22.3
py scripts/package-release.py --version 1.0.0
```

This creates three binary ZIP files, an emulation dependency-source ZIP and `SHA256SUMS.txt` under `dist/releases/v1.0.0`. It checks the PE architecture, pinned source-archive hashes and ZIP contents without executing any protected input. An existing release directory is never overwritten.

```

`docs/EMULATION.md`:

```md
# Emulation mode

`vmp-resource-emulator.exe` executes protected x64 startup code inside Unicorn until the resource table and payloads become available, then uses the shared resource parser to extract them. The target is not launched as a native Windows process.

```text
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.exe --output extracted
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.exe --seconds 300 --instructions 6000000000
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.dll --seconds 600 --instructions 15000000000
```

Defaults are 240 seconds and five billion instructions. The instruction count is a conservative execution budget, not an exact retired-instruction measurement. Unsupported API execution, exceptions and exhausted budgets stop extraction with an error.

DLLs are entered with their image base and `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH`. Modeled system modules are placed outside the input image's address range.

## Windows model

The emulator reads export information and image bytes from the local Windows `ntdll.dll`, `kernel32.dll` and `kernelbase.dll`, plus `user32.dll` when the input imports it. An export being mapped does not mean that its API is implemented. Unmodeled API execution stops the run.

Supported API behavior is modeled in guest memory. Guest file reads are restricted to the input and those modeled system images; guest API requests are not forwarded to Windows.

The model covers the tested startup paths. It does not provide a complete Windows loader or exception dispatcher, and other protection combinations may need additional API or exception handling.

## Resource readiness

For initially empty PE sections, the extractor tracks bytes written by guest code. Every resource payload byte must be backed by file data or an observed write before extraction is accepted. This prevents a table discovered early in startup from producing zero-filled resources while unpacking is still in progress.

This readiness check is not cryptographic validation. It cannot prove that bytes are authentic or account for every possible later decryption pass. Verification against known originals is used for the research fixtures.

## Native helpers

Three native helpers reduce overhead:

- `vmp-native-tracker.dll` tracks memory writes in C++.
- `resource_scanner.dll` searches the mapped image in memory and reuses a discovered table as a hint. A full scan checks for ambiguity before extraction. Each scan has a 20-second budget.
- `block_budget.dll` stops execution slices at translated-block boundaries. It caches decoded instruction counts and checks the exact code bytes to detect self-modifying code. The cache and execution budgets remain bounded.

The optimized path avoids Python callbacks for each write and repeated intermediate images on disk. It retains the resource-readiness checks and writes the final reconstructed image once for extraction.

`--scheduler stock` uses Unicorn's instruction-count scheduler with the other optimizations. `--reference` uses Python write tracking and file-based scans for comparison. Small-block-heavy inputs may benefit less from the block scheduler.

An asynchronous Unicorn timeout is terminal. The interrupted state is not resumed or saved as a reusable checkpoint.

## Verified packed files

| Fixture | Resources | Bytes | Result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| ResourcePaths-x64.vmp.exe | 7 | 152 | Exact match, including names and languages |
| ResourceSample-x64.vmp.exe | 4 | 593 | Exact match: menu, dialog, string table and RCDATA |
| pawdResourceFixture-x64.dll | 7 | 152 | Exact match, including names and languages |

These files had packing and additional protections enabled. All three inputs remained unchanged. The large DLL needs the higher budget shown in the command above. These results describe the tested files, not every VMProtect protection configuration.

## Measurements

| Fixture | Reference | Optimized | Less time |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| ResourcePaths-x64.vmp.exe | 132.6 s | 74.4 s | 44% |
| ResourceSample-x64.vmp.exe | 160.5 s | 83.1 s | 48% |
| pawdResourceFixture-x64.dll | 380.0 s | 199.9 s | 47% |

Measured locally on 2026-11-16 using packaged executables, with the same 600-second and 15-billion-instruction limits. Times cover the full command, including startup and extraction. Optimized values are the median of two sequential runs. Every run matched the original resource bytes and metadata; input hashes stayed unchanged. Results depend on the file and machine.

## Diagnostic output

```text
vmp-resource-emulator.exe protected.exe --snapshot memory.exe
```

`--snapshot` keeps a reconstructed memory image and JSON report after emulation ends. This image is an analysis artifact, not a restored executable. Without this option, only the extracted resources and their manifest are kept.

The source, build scripts and dependency versions are documented in [Building and testing](BUILDING.md) and [third-party notices](../THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md).

```

`docs/PLUGIN.md`:

```md
# Plugin commands

The plugin scans the main EXE unless you supply an address expression for another loaded module.

```text
vre_scan [module-expression]
vre_extract "new output folder"[, candidate-index]
vre_dump "new output folder"[, module-expression][, candidate-index]
```

Square brackets mark optional arguments; do not type them. Candidate indices are zero-based decimal numbers shown in the log. When several tables are found, select one explicitly.

Commands queue background work. `vre_status` prints the current status. Scripts can use `vre_busy()`, `vre_ok()` and `vre_candidates()` to read the job state, last result and table count.

## Capture

Scan after unpacking has finished. A table can become readable before its payloads are ready; the plugin does not track earlier memory writes or prove that resource initialization is complete.

The selected module must have readable PE headers and sections. Capture fails on unreadable or discarded sections rather than filling them with zero bytes. The module-image limit is 256 MiB, with a 20-second budget for capture and scanning.

The plugin copies memory while the target is paused, then extracts from that copy. It leaves the target paused. Resuming execution, switching sessions or loading/unloading a DLL invalidates the scan.

## Static table selection

The standalone extractor can also list and select tables:

```text
vmp-resource-extractor.exe protected.exe --list
vmp-resource-extractor.exe protected.exe --table-rva 0x12345 --key 0x12345678 --output extracted
```

Use the values reported for your file. Hexadecimal arguments need the `0x` prefix. The output folder must be new, and its parent directory must already exist.

```

A. HISTORY OF THE SOFTWARE
==========================

Python was created in the early 1990s by Guido van Rossum at Stichting
Mathematisch Centrum (CWI, see https://www.cwi.nl) in the Netherlands
as a successor of a language called ABC.  Guido remains Python's
principal author, although it includes many contributions from others.

In 1995, Guido continued his work on Python at the Corporation for
National Research Initiatives (CNRI, see https://www.cnri.reston.va.us)
in Reston, Virginia where he released several versions of the
software.

In May 2000, Guido and the Python core development team moved to
BeOpen.com to form the BeOpen PythonLabs team.  In October of the same
year, the PythonLabs team moved to Digital Creations, which became
Zope Corporation.  In 2001, the Python Software Foundation (PSF, see
https://www.python.org/psf/) was formed, a non-profit organization
created specifically to own Python-related Intellectual Property.
Zope Corporation was a sponsoring member of the PSF.

All Python releases are Open Source (see https://opensource.org for
the Open Source Definition).  Historically, most, but not all, Python
releases have also been GPL-compatible; the table below summarizes
the various releases.

    Release         Derived     Year        Owner       GPL-
                    from                                compatible? (1)

    0.9.0 thru 1.2              1991-1995   CWI         yes
    1.3 thru 1.5.2  1.2         1995-1999   CNRI        yes
    1.6             1.5.2       2000        CNRI        no
    2.0             1.6         2000        BeOpen.com  no
    1.6.1           1.6         2001        CNRI        yes (2)
    2.1             2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         no
    2.0.1           2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.1           2.1+2.0.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.2           2.1.1       2002        PSF         yes
    2.1.3           2.1.2       2002        PSF         yes
    2.2 and above   2.1.1       2001-now    PSF         yes

Footnotes:

(1) GPL-compatible doesn't mean that we're distributing Python under
    the GPL.  All Python licenses, unlike the GPL, let you distribute
    a modified version without making your changes open source.  The
    GPL-compatible licenses make it possible to combine Python with
    other software that is released under the GPL; the others don't.

(2) According to Richard Stallman, 1.6.1 is not GPL-compatible,
    because its license has a choice of law clause.  According to
    CNRI, however, Stallman's lawyer has told CNRI's lawyer that 1.6.1
    is "not incompatible" with the GPL.

Thanks to the many outside volunteers who have worked under Guido's
direction to make these releases possible.

                  GNU LIBRARY GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE
                       Version 2, June 1991

 Copyright (C) 1991 Free Software Foundation, Inc.
 51 Franklin Street, Fifth Floor, Boston, MA  02110-1301  USA
 Everyone is permitted to copy and distribute verbatim copies
 of this license document, but changing it is not allowed.

[This is the first released version of the library GPL.  It is
 numbered 2 because it goes with version 2 of the ordinary GPL.]

                            Preamble

  The licenses for most software are designed to take away your
freedom to share and change it.  By contrast, the GNU General Public
Licenses are intended to guarantee your freedom to share and change
free software--to make sure the software is free for all its users.

  This license, the Library General Public License, applies to some
specially designated Free Software Foundation software, and to any
other libraries whose authors decide to use it.  You can use it for
your libraries, too.

  When we speak of free software, we are referring to freedom, not
price.  Our General Public Licenses are designed to make sure that you
have the freedom to distribute copies of free software (and charge for
this service if you wish), that you receive source code or can get it
if you want it, that you can change the software or use pieces of it
in new free programs; and that you know you can do these things.

  To protect your rights, we need to make restrictions that forbid
anyone to deny you these rights or to ask you to surrender the rights.
These restrictions translate to certain responsibilities for you if
you distribute copies of the library, or if you modify it.

  For example, if you distribute copies of the library, whether gratis
or for a fee, you must give the recipients all the rights that we gave
you.  You must make sure that they, too, receive or can get the source
code.  If you link a program with the library, you must provide
complete object files to the recipients so that they can relink them
with the library, after making changes to the library and recompiling
it.  And you must show them these terms so they know their rights.

  Our method of protecting your rights has two steps: (1) copyright
the library, and (2) offer you this license which gives you legal
permission to copy, distribute and/or modify the library.

  Also, for each distributor's protection, we want to make certain
that everyone understands that there is no warranty for this free
library.  If the library is modified by someone else and passed on, we
want its recipients to know that what they have is not the original
version, so that any problems introduced by others will not reflect on
the original authors' reputations.

  Finally, any free program is threatened constantly by software
patents.  We wish to avoid the danger that companies distributing free
software will individually obtain patent licenses, thus in effect
transforming the program into proprietary software.  To prevent this,
we have made it clear that any patent must be licensed for everyone's
free use or not licensed at all.

  Most GNU software, including some libraries, is covered by the ordinary
GNU General Public License, which was designed for utility programs.  This
license, the GNU Library General Public License, applies to certain
designated libraries.  This license is quite different from the ordinary
one; be sure to read it in full, and don't assume that anything in it is
the same as in the ordinary license.

  The reason we have a separate public license for some libraries is that
they blur the distinction we usually make between modifying or adding to a
program and simply using it.  Linking a program with a library, without
changing the library, is in some sense simply using the library, and is
analogous to running a utility program or application program.  However, in
a textual and legal sense, the linked executable is a combined work, a
derivative of the original library, and the ordinary General Public License
treats it as such.

  Because of this blurred distinction, using the ordinary General
Public License for libraries did not effectively promote software
sharing, because most developers did not use the libraries.  We
concluded that weaker conditions might promote sharing better.

  However, unrestricted linking of non-free programs would deprive the
users of those programs of all benefit from the free status of the
libraries themselves.  This Library General Public License is intended to
permit developers of non-free programs to use free libraries, while
preserving your freedom as a user of such programs to change the free
libraries that are incorporated in them.  (We have not seen how to achieve
this as regards changes in header files, but we have achieved it as regards
changes in the actual functions of the Library.)  The hope is that this
will lead to faster development of free libraries.

  The precise terms and conditions for copying, distribution and
modification follow.  Pay close attention to the difference between a
"work based on the library" and a "work that uses the library".  The
former contains code derived from the library, while the latter only
works together with the library.

  Note that it is possible for a library to be covered by the ordinary
General Public License rather than by this special one.

which has been distributed under these terms.  A "work based on the

making modifications to it.  For a library, complete source code means
all the source code for all modules it contains, plus any associated
interface definition files, plus the scripts used to control compilation
and installation of the library.

covered by this License; they are outside its scope.  The act of
running a program using the Library is not restricted, and output from
such a program is covered only if its contents constitute a work based
on the Library (independent of the use of the Library in a tool for
writing it).  Whether that is true depends on what the Library does
and what the program that uses the Library does.

    be optional: if the application does not supply it, the square

These requirements apply to the modified work as a whole.  If
identifiable sections of that work are not derived from the Library,

themselves, then this License, and its terms, do not apply to those

your rights to work written entirely by you; rather, the intent is to
exercise the right to control the distribution of derivative or
collective works based on the Library.

must be distributed under the terms of Sections 1 and 2 above on a

  If distribution of object code is made by offering access to copy
from a designated place, then offering equivalent access to copy the
source code from the same place satisfies the requirement to
distribute the source code, even though third parties are not
compelled to copy the source along with the object code.

  5. A program that contains no derivative of any portion of the
Library, but is designed to work with the Library by being compiled or
linked with it, is called a "work that uses the Library".  Such a
work, in isolation, is not a derivative work of the Library, and
therefore falls outside the scope of this License.

  However, linking a "work that uses the Library" with the Library
creates an executable that is a derivative of the Library (because it
contains portions of the Library), rather than a "work that uses the
library".  The executable is therefore covered by this License.
Section 6 states terms for distribution of such executables.

  When a "work that uses the Library" uses material from a header file
that is part of the Library, the object code for the work may be a
derivative work of the Library even though the source code is not.
Whether this is true is especially significant if the work can be
linked without the Library, or if the work is itself a library.  The
threshold for this to be true is not precisely defined by law.

  If such an object file uses only numerical parameters, data
structure layouts and accessors, and small macros and small inline
functions (ten lines or less in length), then the use of the object
file is unrestricted, regardless of whether it is legally a derivative
work.  (Executables containing this object code plus portions of the
Library will still fall under Section 6.)

  Otherwise, if the work is a derivative of the Library, you may
distribute the object code for the work under the terms of Section 6.
Any executables containing that work also fall under Section 6,
whether or not they are linked directly with the Library itself.

Library" must include any data and utility programs needed for
reproducing the executable from it.  However, as a special exception,
the source code distributed need not include anything that is normally
distributed (in either source or binary form) with the major

		  GNU LIBRARY GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE
		       Version 2, June 1991

 Copyright (C) 1991 Free Software Foundation, Inc.

[This is the first released version of the library GPL.  It is
 numbered 2 because it goes with version 2 of the ordinary GPL.]

			    Preamble

  The licenses for most software are designed to take away your
freedom to share and change it.  By contrast, the GNU General Public
Licenses are intended to guarantee your freedom to share and change
free software--to make sure the software is free for all its users.

  This license, the Library General Public License, applies to some
specially designated Free Software Foundation software, and to any
other libraries whose authors decide to use it.  You can use it for
your libraries, too.

  When we speak of free software, we are referring to freedom, not
price.  Our General Public Licenses are designed to make sure that you
have the freedom to distribute copies of free software (and charge for
this service if you wish), that you receive source code or can get it
if you want it, that you can change the software or use pieces of it
in new free programs; and that you know you can do these things.

  To protect your rights, we need to make restrictions that forbid
anyone to deny you these rights or to ask you to surrender the rights.
These restrictions translate to certain responsibilities for you if
you distribute copies of the library, or if you modify it.

  For example, if you distribute copies of the library, whether gratis
or for a fee, you must give the recipients all the rights that we gave
you.  You must make sure that they, too, receive or can get the source
code.  If you link a program with the library, you must provide
complete object files to the recipients so that they can relink them
with the library, after making changes to the library and recompiling
it.  And you must show them these terms so they know their rights.

  Our method of protecting your rights has two steps: (1) copyright
the library, and (2) offer you this license which gives you legal
permission to copy, distribute and/or modify the library.

  Also, for each distributor's protection, we want to make certain
that everyone understands that there is no warranty for this free
library.  If the library is modified by someone else and passed on, we
want its recipients to know that what they have is not the original
version, so that any problems introduced by others will not reflect on
the original authors' reputations.

  Finally, any free program is threatened constantly by software
patents.  We wish to avoid the danger that companies distributing free
software will individually obtain patent licenses, thus in effect
transforming the program into proprietary software.  To prevent this,
we have made it clear that any patent must be licensed for everyone's
free use or not licensed at all.

  Most GNU software, including some libraries, is covered by the ordinary
GNU General Public License, which was designed for utility programs.  This
license, the GNU Library General Public License, applies to certain
designated libraries.  This license is quite different from the ordinary
one; be sure to read it in full, and don't assume that anything in it is
the same as in the ordinary license.

  The reason we have a separate public license for some libraries is that
they blur the distinction we usually make between modifying or adding to a
program and simply using it.  Linking a program with a library, without
changing the library, is in some sense simply using the library, and is
analogous to running a utility program or application program.  However, in
a textual and legal sense, the linked executable is a combined work, a
derivative of the original library, and the ordinary General Public License
treats it as such.

  Because of this blurred distinction, using the ordinary General
Public License for libraries did not effectively promote software
sharing, because most developers did not use the libraries.  We
concluded that weaker conditions might promote sharing better.

  However, unrestricted linking of non-free programs would deprive the
users of those programs of all benefit from the free status of the
libraries themselves.  This Library General Public License is intended to
permit developers of non-free programs to use free libraries, while
preserving your freedom as a user of such programs to change the free
libraries that are incorporated in them.  (We have not seen how to achieve
this as regards changes in header files, but we have achieved it as regards
changes in the actual functions of the Library.)  The hope is that this
will lead to faster development of free libraries.

  The precise terms and conditions for copying, distribution and
modification follow.  Pay close attention to the difference between a
"work based on the library" and a "work that uses the library".  The
former contains code derived from the library, while the latter only
works together with the library.

  Note that it is possible for a library to be covered by the ordinary
General Public License rather than by this special one.

`licenses/pyinstaller-license.txt`:

```txt
================================
 The PyInstaller licensing terms
================================
 

Copyright (c) 2010-2023, PyInstaller Development Team
Copyright (c) 2005-2009, Giovanni Bajo
Based on previous work under copyright (c) 2002 McMillan Enterprises, Inc.


PyInstaller is licensed under the terms of the GNU General Public License
as published by the Free Software Foundation; either version 2 of the License,
or (at your option) any later version.


Bootloader Exception
--------------------

In addition to the permissions in the GNU General Public License, the
authors give you unlimited permission to link or embed compiled bootloader
and related files into combinations with other programs, and to distribute
those combinations without any restriction coming from the use of those
files. (The General Public License restrictions do apply in other respects;
for example, they cover modification of the files, and distribution when
not linked into a combined executable.)
 
 
Bootloader and Related Files
----------------------------

Bootloader and related files are files which are embedded within the
final executable. This includes files in directories:

./bootloader/
./PyInstaller/loader


Run-time Hooks
----------------------------

Run-time Hooks are a different kind of files embedded within the final
executable. To ease moving them into a separate repository, or into the
respective project, these files are now licensed under the Apache License,
Version 2.0.

Run-time Hooks are in the directory
./PyInstaller/hooks/rthooks


Additional Run-time Modules
----------------------------------

In addition to run-time hooks, PyInstaller may embed additional run-time
modules in the final executable. At the time of writing, these include
the splash-screen module (``pyi_splash``) and modules from utility package
for run-time hooks (``_pyi_rth_utils``). All these files are licensed under
the Apache License, Version 2.0, and are located in the following directory:

./PyInstaller/fake-modules


The PyInstaller.isolated submodule
----------------------------------

By request, the PyInstaller.isolated submodule and its corresponding tests are
additionally licensed with the MIT license so that it may be reused outside of
PyInstaller under GPL 2.0 or MIT terms and conditions -- whichever is the most
suitable to the recipient downstream project. Affected files/directories are:

./PyInstaller/isolated/
./tests/unit/test_isolation.py


About the PyInstaller Development Team
--------------------------------------

The PyInstaller Development Team is the set of contributors
to the PyInstaller project. A full list with details is kept
in the documentation directory, in the file
``doc/CREDITS.rst``.

The core team that coordinates development on GitHub can be found here:
https://github.com/pyinstaller/pyinstaller.  As of 2021, it consists of:

* Hartmut Goebel
* Jasper Harrison
* Bryan Jones
* Brenainn Woodsend
* Rok Mandeljc

Our Copyright Policy
--------------------

PyInstaller uses a shared copyright model. Each contributor maintains copyright
over their contributions to PyInstaller. But, it is important to note that these
contributions are typically only changes to the repositories. Thus,
the PyInstaller source code, in its entirety is not the copyright of any single
person or institution.  Instead, it is the collective copyright of the entire
PyInstaller Development Team.  If individual contributors want to maintain
a record of what changes/contributions they have specific copyright on, they
should indicate their copyright in the commit message of the change, when they
commit the change to the PyInstaller repository.

With this in mind, the following banner should be used in any source code file
to indicate the copyright and license terms:


#-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Copyright (c) 2005-2023, PyInstaller Development Team.
#
# Distributed under the terms of the GNU General Public License (version 2
# or later) with exception for distributing the bootloader.
#
# The full license is in the file COPYING.txt, distributed with this software.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: (GPL-2.0-or-later WITH Bootloader-exception)
#-----------------------------------------------------------------------------


For run-time hooks, the following banner should be used:

`plugin/README.txt`:

```txt
VMProtect Resource Extractor - x64dbg plugin

Install
Copy VMPResourceExtractor.dp64 into x64dbg's x64/plugins folder,
then start x64dbg. This build supports x64 targets only.
No kernel driver, Python or emulator installation is needed.

Use
1. Load or attach to the program. Let it finish unpacking first.
2. Open Plugins > VMP Resource Extractor > Extract resources...
3. Select the main EXE or a loaded DLL. Refresh updates the list.
4. Click Scan. A running target is paused and stays paused.
5. Select a table if several were found. Choose a new output
   folder and click Extract. Use F9 to continue the target.

For a DLL, wait until its host has loaded and initialized it.
Browse chooses a parent folder and suggests an unused child folder.
Existing output folders are never overwritten, even if empty.

Output
Numbered .bin files contain the raw resource data. manifest.json
records type, ID or name, language, codepage, size, payload RVA,
module base and capture method. Dialogs, string tables and other
resource types retain their native format.

Commands
vre_ui
vre_scan
vre_extract "C:\work\extracted"
vre_dump "C:\work\extracted-next"
vre_status

Optional arguments (do not type the square brackets):
vre_scan [module-expression]
vre_extract "new output folder"[, candidate-index]
vre_dump "new output folder"[, module-expression][, candidate-index]

The default module is the main EXE. A module expression can specify
an address inside another loaded module. Candidate indices are
zero-based decimal numbers printed in the x64dbg log.

Commands run in the background. vre_status reports the result.
Scripts can query vre_busy(), vre_ok() and vre_candidates().

Notes
The plugin reads target memory without guest hooks, code patches
or breakpoint changes. Resuming the target or changing loaded
modules invalidates a scan; scan again before extracting.

A valid table does not prove unpacking has finished. Scan after
the resources are initialized. Unreadable or discarded sections
and missing PE headers can prevent capture. The module-image limit
is 256 MiB; capture and scanning have a 20-second budget.

This extracts the private encrypted resource tree. It does not
rebuild .rsrc, restore a runnable EXE or bypass anti-debug checks.

Ivan Kozlovsky
ivankozlovsky.com
contact@ivankozlovsky.com

```

`plugin/backend.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "backend.h"
#define RESOURCE_SCANNER_LIBRARY
#include "../standalone/main.cpp"
#include <atomic>
#include <cstring>
#include <limits>

namespace vre {
namespace {
constexpr uint64_t MaxImage=256ull*1024*1024;
constexpr uint64_t MaxHeaders=1024*1024;
constexpr uint64_t Page=4096;

uint16_t read16(const Bytes& bytes,size_t offset) {
    if(offset>bytes.size() || 2>bytes.size()-offset) throw Invalid("Truncated memory image header");
    return uint16_t(bytes[offset]) | uint16_t(bytes[offset+1])<<8;
}
uint32_t read32(const Bytes& bytes,size_t offset) {
    if(offset>bytes.size() || 4>bytes.size()-offset) throw Invalid("Truncated memory image header");
    uint32_t value=0;
    for(unsigned i=0;i<4;i++) value|=uint32_t(bytes[offset+i])<<(i*8);
    return value;
}
void write32(Bytes& bytes,size_t offset,uint32_t value) {
    if(offset>bytes.size() || 4>bytes.size()-offset) throw Invalid("Truncated memory image header");
    for(unsigned i=0;i<4;i++) bytes[offset+i]=uint8_t(value>>(i*8));
}
void check_state(const IsValid& valid,const ScanDeadline& deadline) {
    deadline.check(true);
    if(!valid()) throw Invalid("Debugger state changed during capture. Pause the target and scan again");
}
void read_range(const Module& selected,const ReadMemory& read,const IsValid& valid,
                const ScanDeadline& deadline,uint64_t rva,void* destination,size_t size) {
    if(rva>selected.size || uint64_t(size)>selected.size-rva) throw Invalid("Memory read exceeds the selected module");
    auto output=static_cast<uint8_t*>(destination);
    size_t done=0;
    while(done<size) {
        check_state(valid,deadline);
        const uint64_t current=selected.base+rva+done;
        const size_t count=std::min(size-done,size_t(Page-(current&(Page-1))));
        if(!read(current,output+done,count)) {
            throw Invalid("Cannot read module memory at "+hex(current)+
                ". An image section may be inaccessible or discarded; no incomplete snapshot was accepted");
        }
        done+=count;
    }
}

fs::path create_staging(const fs::path& output) {
    static std::atomic<uint64_t> sequence{0};
    for(unsigned attempt=0;attempt<32;attempt++) {
        fs::path stage=output;
        stage+=L".partial-vre-"+std::to_wstring(GetCurrentProcessId())+L"-"+
               std::to_wstring(GetTickCount64())+L"-"+std::to_wstring(sequence.fetch_add(1));
        if(fs::create_directory(stage)) return stage;
    }
    throw Invalid("Cannot reserve a temporary output directory");
}
void cleanup_staging(const fs::path& stage,const fs::path& parent) noexcept {
    // The stage was created by this operation, as a direct sibling of output.
    // remove_all removes reparse-point links themselves rather than their target.
    try {
        if(fs::weakly_canonical(stage.parent_path())!=fs::weakly_canonical(parent)) return;
        std::error_code error;
        fs::remove_all(stage,error);
    } catch(...) {}
}
}

class Snapshot {
public:
    Module selected;
    std::unique_ptr<PE> image;
    std::vector<Table> tables;
    std::vector<Candidate> summaries;
};

SnapshotPtr capture(const Module& selected,const ReadMemory& read,const IsValid& valid) {
    if(!read || !valid) throw Invalid("Memory reader and debugger state validator are required");
    if(!selected.base || selected.size<64 || selected.size>MaxImage ||
       selected.size-1>std::numeric_limits<uint64_t>::max()-selected.base)
        throw Invalid("Selected module has an invalid range or exceeds the 256 MiB image limit");
    const ScanDeadline deadline(20000);
    check_state(valid,deadline);

    Bytes dos(64);
    read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,0,dos.data(),dos.size());
    if(read16(dos,0)!=IMAGE_DOS_SIGNATURE) throw Invalid("Selected module has no readable DOS/PE headers");
    const uint64_t nt=read32(dos,0x3c);
    if(nt>MaxHeaders || nt>selected.size || 24>selected.size-nt) throw Invalid("Invalid PE header offset");
    Bytes prefix(size_t(nt+24));
    read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,0,prefix.data(),prefix.size());
    if(prefix.size()<dos.size() || !std::equal(dos.begin(),dos.end(),prefix.begin()))
        throw Invalid("Module headers changed during capture; scan again while paused");
    if(read32(prefix,size_t(nt))!=IMAGE_NT_SIGNATURE) throw Invalid("Invalid PE signature in target memory");
    if(read16(prefix,size_t(nt+4))!=IMAGE_FILE_MACHINE_AMD64) throw Invalid("This plugin supports x64 modules only");
    const uint32_t count=read16(prefix,size_t(nt+6));
    const uint32_t optSize=read16(prefix,size_t(nt+20));
    if(!count || count>96 || optSize<112 || optSize>4096) throw Invalid("Invalid PE optional header or section count");
    const uint64_t optional=nt+24, sectionTable=optional+optSize;
    const uint64_t minimumHeaders=sectionTable+uint64_t(count)*40;
    if(minimumHeaders>selected.size || minimumHeaders>MaxHeaders) throw Invalid("PE section headers exceed the module");
    Bytes headers(static_cast<size_t>(minimumHeaders));
    read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,0,headers.data(),headers.size());
    if(!std::equal(prefix.begin(),prefix.end(),headers.begin()))
        throw Invalid("Module headers changed during capture; scan again while paused");
    if(read16(headers,size_t(optional))!=IMAGE_NT_OPTIONAL_HDR64_MAGIC) throw Invalid("This plugin requires PE32+ headers");
    const uint32_t imageSize=read32(headers,size_t(optional+56));
    const uint32_t headerSize=read32(headers,size_t(optional+60));
    if(!imageSize || imageSize>selected.size || imageSize>MaxImage ||
       headerSize<minimumHeaders || headerSize>imageSize || headerSize>MaxHeaders)
        throw Invalid("Invalid SizeOfImage or SizeOfHeaders in target memory");
    Bytes completeHeaders(headerSize);
    read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,0,completeHeaders.data(),completeHeaders.size());
    if(!std::equal(headers.begin(),headers.end(),completeHeaders.begin()))
        throw Invalid("Module headers changed during capture; scan again while paused");
    headers=std::move(completeHeaders);

    struct Span {uint32_t rva,size;size_t header;};
    std::vector<Span> spans;
    for(uint32_t i=0;i<count;i++) {
        const size_t offset=size_t(sectionTable)+size_t(i)*40;
        const uint32_t rva=read32(headers,offset+12);
        const uint32_t length=std::max(read32(headers,offset+8),read32(headers,offset+16));
        if(!length) continue;
        if(rva<headerSize || rva>imageSize || length>imageSize-rva) throw Invalid("Section range exceeds the mapped image");
        for(const auto& span:spans)
            if(uint64_t(rva)<uint64_t(span.rva)+span.size && uint64_t(span.rva)<uint64_t(rva)+length)
                throw Invalid("Overlapping virtual sections in target memory");
        spans.push_back({rva,length,offset});
    }
    if(spans.empty()) throw Invalid("Selected module has no mapped sections");
    Bytes bytes(imageSize,0);
    std::copy(headers.begin(),headers.end(),bytes.begin());
    for(const auto& span:spans) {
        read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,span.rva,bytes.data()+span.rva,span.size);
        // Normalize the captured image for the same parser used by standalone.
        write32(bytes,span.header+16,span.size);
        write32(bytes,span.header+20,span.rva);
    }
    Bytes after(headers.size());
    read_range(selected,read,valid,deadline,0,after.data(),after.size());
    if(after!=headers) throw Invalid("Module headers changed during capture; scan again while paused");
    check_state(valid,deadline);
    auto snapshot=std::make_shared<Snapshot>();
    snapshot->selected=selected;
    snapshot->selected.size=imageSize;
    snapshot->image=std::make_unique<PE>(std::move(bytes));
    snapshot->tables=detect(*snapshot->image,&deadline);
    check_state(valid,deadline);
    for(const auto& table:snapshot->tables)
        snapshot->summaries.push_back({table.rva,table.key,table.resources.size(),table.total});
    return snapshot;
}

const Module& module(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot) {
    if(!snapshot) throw Invalid("No memory snapshot is selected");
    return snapshot->selected;
}
const std::vector<Candidate>& candidates(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot) {
    if(!snapshot) throw Invalid("No memory snapshot is selected");
    return snapshot->summaries;
}

ExtractResult extract_snapshot(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot,size_t candidateIndex,const fs::path& output) {
    if(!snapshot || candidateIndex>=snapshot->tables.size()) throw Invalid("Select a resource table from a completed scan");
    const fs::path destination=fs::absolute(output).lexically_normal();
    if(destination.filename().empty() || !fs::is_directory(destination.parent_path()))
        throw Invalid("Choose a new output folder beneath an existing directory");
    if(fs::exists(destination)) throw Invalid("Output folder already exists; choose a new folder");
    const fs::path stage=create_staging(destination), content=stage/L"resources";
    const auto& table=snapshot->tables[candidateIndex];
    try {
        fs::path input=snapshot->selected.path.empty()?snapshot->selected.name:snapshot->selected.path;
        if(input.empty()) input=L"module";
        ::extract(*snapshot->image,table,input,content);
        const auto manifestPath=content/L"manifest.json";
        std::ifstream source(manifestPath,std::ios::binary);
        std::string manifest((std::istreambuf_iterator<char>(source)),{});
        if(!source || manifest.size()<2 || manifest[0]!='{') throw Invalid("Cannot read staged resource manifest");
        source.close();
        const std::string origin="\n  \"method\": \"x64dbg-memory\",\n  \"module_base\": "+quote(hex(snapshot->selected.base))+
            ",\n  \"capture\": \"copied while paused; structural validation, payload initialization is not proven\",";
        manifest.insert(1,origin);
        writeFile(manifestPath,manifest.data(),manifest.size());
        // MoveFileW never replaces even an empty destination created meanwhile.
        if(!MoveFileW(content.c_str(),destination.c_str()))
            throw Invalid("Cannot publish resource output (Windows error "+std::to_string(GetLastError())+
                          "). The destination may already exist");
        cleanup_staging(stage,destination.parent_path());
        return {destination,table.resources.size(),table.total};
    } catch(...) {
        cleanup_staging(stage,destination.parent_path());
        throw;
    }
}
}

```

`plugin/backend.h`:

```h
#pragma once
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <filesystem>
#include <functional>
#include <memory>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

namespace vre {
struct Module {
    uint64_t base=0;
    uint64_t size=0;
    std::wstring name;
    std::wstring path;
};
struct Candidate {
    uint32_t tableRva=0;
    uint32_t key=0;
    size_t resources=0;
    uint64_t bytes=0;
};
class Snapshot;
using SnapshotPtr=std::shared_ptr<Snapshot>;
using ReadMemory=std::function<bool(uint64_t,void*,size_t)>;
using IsValid=std::function<bool()>;

// The caller keeps the selected debug session paused throughout capture.
// A snapshot contains copied image bytes and never reads the target on export.
SnapshotPtr capture(const Module& selected,const ReadMemory& read,const IsValid& valid);
const Module& module(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot);
const std::vector<Candidate>& candidates(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot);

struct ExtractResult {
    std::filesystem::path output;
    size_t resources=0;
    uint64_t bytes=0;
};
ExtractResult extract_snapshot(const SnapshotPtr& snapshot,size_t candidateIndex,
                               const std::filesystem::path& output);
}

`plugin/plugin.cpp`:

```cpp
#define NOMINMAX
#include <windows.h>
#include <objbase.h>
#include "_plugins.h"
#include "_scriptapi_module.h"
#include "backend.h"
#include "ui.h"
#include <algorithm>
#include <atomic>
#include <chrono>
#include <condition_variable>
#include <cstring>
#include <filesystem>
#include <functional>
#include <iomanip>
#include <mutex>
#include <sstream>
#include <thread>

namespace {
HINSTANCE instance=nullptr;
int pluginHandle=0;
std::atomic<bool> stopping=false,busy=false;
std::atomic<uint64_t> generation=1,pauses=0;
std::atomic<HWND> dispatcher=nullptr;
std::thread worker,uiThread;
std::mutex stateMutex,jobMutex;
std::condition_variable jobWake;
std::function<void()> queuedJob;
vreui::ViewState view;
vre::SnapshotPtr snapshot;
uint64_t snapshotGeneration=0;
DWORD snapshotPid=0;
bool lastOk=false;
std::wstring lastOutput;
constexpr UINT Show=WM_APP+41,Update=WM_APP+42,Shutdown=WM_APP+43;
constexpr wchar_t WindowClass[]=L"VMPResourceExtractor.Dispatcher";

std::wstring wide(const std::string& s) {
    if(s.empty()) return {};
    int n=MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8,MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS,s.data(),int(s.size()),nullptr,0);
    if(!n) throw std::runtime_error("Invalid UTF-8 text");
    std::wstring r(n,L'\0'); MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8,0,s.data(),int(s.size()),r.data(),n); return r;
}
std::wstring wideError(const char* text) {
    if(!text) return L"Unknown failure";
    int n=MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8,0,text,-1,nullptr,0);
    if(n<=1) return L"Unknown failure";
    std::wstring out(size_t(n),L'\0');
    MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8,0,text,-1,out.data(),n);out.pop_back();return out;
}
std::string narrow(const std::wstring& s) {
    if(s.empty()) return {};
    int n=WideCharToMultiByte(CP_UTF8,WC_ERR_INVALID_CHARS,s.data(),int(s.size()),nullptr,0,nullptr,nullptr);
    if(!n) return "Invalid text";
    std::string r(n,'\0'); WideCharToMultiByte(CP_UTF8,0,s.data(),int(s.size()),r.data(),n,nullptr,nullptr); return r;
}
std::wstring hex(uint64_t v) {std::wostringstream s;s<<L"0x"<<std::hex<<std::uppercase<<v;return s.str();}
void notify() {if(HWND w=dispatcher.load()) PostMessageW(w,Update,0,0);}
void status(const std::wstring& text,bool log=true) {
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);view.status=text;}
    notify(); if(log) _plugin_logprintf("[VRE] %s\n",narrow(text).c_str());
}
void clearSnapshotLocked() {snapshot.reset();snapshotGeneration=0;snapshotPid=0;view.candidates.clear();view.selectedCandidate=SIZE_MAX;}
void invalidate(const wchar_t* message,bool clearModules=false) {
    ++generation;
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);clearSnapshotLocked();lastOk=false;
     if(clearModules){view.modules.clear();view.selectedModule=0;}
     if(!busy) view.status=message;}
    notify();
}
vre::Module moduleAt(uint64_t address) {
    Script::Module::ModuleInfo m{};
    bool ok=address?Script::Module::InfoFromAddr(duint(address),&m):Script::Module::GetMainModuleInfo(&m);
    if(!DbgIsDebugging()||!ok) throw std::runtime_error("No loaded module selected");
    return {m.base,m.size,wide(m.name),wide(m.path)};
}
std::wstring newOutput(const vre::Module& m) {
    SYSTEMTIME t{};GetLocalTime(&t);
    wchar_t suffix[80]{};swprintf_s(suffix,L".resources-%04u%02u%02u-%02u%02u%02u",t.wYear,t.wMonth,t.wDay,t.wHour,t.wMinute,t.wSecond);
    std::filesystem::path path(m.path);
    if(path.empty()) path=std::filesystem::current_path()/m.name;
    path+=suffix;
    auto initial=path;
    for(unsigned i=1;std::filesystem::exists(path);++i) {path=initial;path+=L"-"+std::to_wstring(i);}
    return path.wstring();
}
void refreshModules() {
    std::vector<vreui::ModuleChoice> choices;
    uint64_t main=0;
    if(DbgIsDebugging()) {
        main=Script::Module::GetMainModuleBase();
        BridgeList<Script::Module::ModuleInfo> modules;
        if(!Script::Module::GetList(&modules)) throw std::runtime_error("Could not list loaded modules");
        for(int i=0;i<modules.Count();++i) {
            const auto& m=modules[i];
            choices.push_back({wide(m.name)+L"  ["+hex(m.base)+L"]"+(m.base==main?L"  (main)":L""),m.base});
        }
        std::stable_sort(choices.begin(),choices.end(),[main](const auto&a,const auto&b){return (a.base==main)>(b.base==main);});
    }
    uint64_t selected=0;bool needOutput=false;
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);
     view.modules=std::move(choices);
     auto match=std::find_if(view.modules.begin(),view.modules.end(),[](const auto& m){return m.base==view.selectedModule;});
     if(match==view.modules.end()){view.selectedModule=view.modules.empty()?0:view.modules.front().base;clearSnapshotLocked();}
     selected=view.selectedModule;needOutput=view.output.empty();}
    if(selected&&needOutput){auto value=newOutput(moduleAt(selected));std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);if(view.output.empty())view.output=value;}
    notify();
}
void pauseForCapture() {
    if(!DbgIsDebugging()) throw std::runtime_error("Load or attach to a program first");
    if(!DbgIsRunning()) return;
    const DWORD pid=DbgGetProcessId(); const uint64_t ticket=pauses.load();
    status(L"Pausing the target for a consistent memory snapshot...");
    // This runs on our worker, outside x64dbg's command callback. Execute the
    // pause request here so capture does not depend on command-queue progress.
    if(!DbgCmdExecDirect("pause")) throw std::runtime_error("Could not request pause");
    const auto until=std::chrono::steady_clock::now()+std::chrono::seconds(8);
    while(std::chrono::steady_clock::now()<until) {
        if(stopping||!DbgIsDebugging()||DbgGetProcessId()!=pid) throw std::runtime_error("Debug session changed while pausing");
        if(!DbgIsRunning()&&pauses.load()>ticket) return;
        std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::milliseconds(20));
    }
    throw std::runtime_error("Pause did not complete. Pause manually, then scan again");
}
void scan(uint64_t selected) {
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);clearSnapshotLocked();}
    pauseForCapture(); refreshModules();
    const auto epoch=generation.load(); const DWORD pid=DbgGetProcessId();
    auto valid=[epoch,pid] {return !stopping&&generation.load()==epoch&&DbgIsDebugging()&&!DbgIsRunning()&&DbgGetProcessId()==pid;};
    if(!valid())throw std::runtime_error("Debug session changed before capture");
    const auto mod=moduleAt(selected);
    if(!valid())throw std::runtime_error("Debug session changed while selecting the module");
    status(L"Reading and scanning "+mod.name+L"...");
    auto found=vre::capture(mod,[](uint64_t a,void* p,size_t n){return DbgMemRead(duint(a),p,duint(n));},valid);
    if(!valid()) throw std::runtime_error("Target resumed or changed during capture. Scan again while paused");
    auto items=vre::candidates(found);
    if(items.empty()) throw std::runtime_error("No supported resource table found. Run past unpacking, pause and scan again");
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);
     if(generation.load()!=epoch) throw std::runtime_error("Debug session changed during capture");
     snapshot=found;snapshotGeneration=epoch;snapshotPid=pid;view.selectedModule=mod.base;
     view.candidates.clear();
     for(size_t i=0;i<items.size();++i){const auto& c=items[i];view.candidates.push_back({hex(c.tableRva)+L"  |  "+std::to_wstring(c.resources)+L" resources  |  "+std::to_wstring(c.bytes)+L" bytes",i});}
     view.selectedCandidate=items.size()==1?0:SIZE_MAX;
     if(view.output.empty()) view.output=newOutput(mod);}
    status(L"Found "+std::to_wstring(items.size())+L" table(s). Select a table and extract. Target remains paused.");
    for(size_t i=0;i<items.size();++i) _plugin_logprintf("[VRE] candidate=%llu table=%llX key=%08X resources=%llu bytes=%llu\n",
       static_cast<unsigned long long>(i),static_cast<unsigned long long>(items[i].tableRva),items[i].key,
       static_cast<unsigned long long>(items[i].resources),static_cast<unsigned long long>(items[i].bytes));
}
void extract(uint64_t selected,size_t index,const std::wstring& output) {
    vre::SnapshotPtr copy;
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);
     if(!snapshot||snapshotGeneration!=generation.load()||snapshotPid!=DbgGetProcessId()||!DbgIsDebugging()||DbgIsRunning())
       throw std::runtime_error("No current paused snapshot. Scan the module again");
     if(selected&&vre::module(snapshot).base!=selected) throw std::runtime_error("Selected module changed. Scan it first");
     copy=snapshot;}
    if(output.empty()) throw std::runtime_error("Choose a new output directory");
    const auto& choices=vre::candidates(copy);
    if(index==SIZE_MAX) {if(choices.size()!=1) throw std::runtime_error("Multiple tables found. Select a table explicitly");index=0;}
    status(L"Extracting resources from the captured snapshot...");
    auto result=vre::extract_snapshot(copy,index,std::filesystem::path(output));
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);lastOutput=result.output.wstring();view.output=lastOutput;}
    status(L"Extracted "+std::to_wstring(result.resources)+L" resources ("+std::to_wstring(result.bytes)+L" bytes).\n"+result.output.wstring());
}
bool submit(std::function<void()> job) {
    if(stopping||busy.exchange(true)){_plugin_logputs("[VRE] An operation is already in progress or the plugin is stopping.");return false;}
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);view.busy=true;lastOk=false;}
    const auto epoch=generation.load();
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(jobMutex);queuedJob=[epoch,job=std::move(job)]{
        if(stopping||generation.load()!=epoch)throw std::runtime_error("Debug session changed before the operation started");job();};}
    notify();jobWake.notify_one();return true;
}
void workerMain() {
    for(;;) {
        std::function<void()> job;
        {std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(jobMutex);jobWake.wait(lock,[]{return stopping||bool(queuedJob);});
         if(stopping) break;job=std::move(queuedJob);queuedJob={};}
        bool ok=false;
        try {
            try {job();ok=true;} catch(const std::exception& e) {status(L"Error: "+wideError(e.what()));}
            catch(...) {status(L"Error: unexpected extraction failure");}
        } catch(...) {_plugin_logputs("[VRE] Operation failed; could not format the error message.");}
        {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);view.busy=false;lastOk=ok;}
        busy=false;notify();
    }
}
void moduleChanged(uint64_t base) {
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);
     if(base!=view.selectedModule){view.selectedModule=base;clearSnapshotLocked();view.status=L"Module changed. Scan after unpacking.";}}
    notify();
}
LRESULT CALLBACK dispatcherProc(HWND w,UINT msg,WPARAM wp,LPARAM lp) {
    if(msg==Show&&!stopping) {vreui::show();msg=Update;}
    if(msg==Update&&!stopping) {vreui::ViewState copy;{std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);copy=view;}vreui::update(copy);return 0;}
    if(msg==Shutdown) {vreui::close();DestroyWindow(w);PostQuitMessage(0);return 0;}
    return DefWindowProcW(w,msg,wp,lp);
}
void uiMain() {
    CoInitializeEx(nullptr,COINIT_APARTMENTTHREADED);
    WNDCLASSW cls{};cls.lpfnWndProc=dispatcherProc;cls.hInstance=instance;cls.lpszClassName=WindowClass;
    if(!RegisterClassW(&cls)){_plugin_logputs("[VRE] Could not register the UI dispatcher.");CoUninitialize();return;}
    HWND window=CreateWindowExW(0,WindowClass,L"",0,0,0,0,0,HWND_MESSAGE,nullptr,instance,nullptr);
    if(!window){UnregisterClassW(WindowClass,instance);CoUninitialize();return;}
    vreui::Callbacks callbacks;
    callbacks.refresh=[]{submit([]{refreshModules();status(L"Modules refreshed. Run until unpacking finishes, then scan.");});};
    callbacks.scan=[](uint64_t m){submit([m]{scan(m);});};
    callbacks.extract=[](uint64_t m,size_t i,const std::wstring& p){submit([m,i,p]{extract(m,i,p);});};
    callbacks.moduleChanged=moduleChanged;
    callbacks.outputChanged=[](const std::wstring& p){std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);view.output=p;};
    vreui::initialize(instance,nullptr,std::move(callbacks));dispatcher=window;
    if(stopping)PostMessageW(window,Shutdown,0,0);
    MSG message{};
    while(GetMessageW(&message,nullptr,0,0)>0){if(!vreui::translate(&message)){TranslateMessage(&message);DispatchMessageW(&message);}}
    dispatcher=nullptr;vreui::close();UnregisterClassW(WindowClass,instance);CoUninitialize();
}
uint64_t argumentModule(int argc,char** argv,int pos) {
    if(argc<=pos) return 0;
    duint value=0;if(!DbgIsValidExpression(argv[pos])||!DbgValFromString(argv[pos])) throw std::runtime_error("Invalid module expression");
    value=DbgValFromString(argv[pos]);return value;
}
size_t argumentIndex(int argc,char** argv,int pos) {
    if(argc<=pos) return SIZE_MAX;
    if(!argv[pos][0]||argv[pos][0]=='-')throw std::runtime_error("Candidate index must be a nonnegative decimal number");
    size_t used=0;auto value=std::stoull(argv[pos],&used,10);
    if(used!=strlen(argv[pos])) throw std::runtime_error("Candidate index must be a decimal number");return size_t(value);
}
bool commandScan(int argc,char** argv) {try{auto m=argumentModule(argc,argv,1);return submit([m]{scan(m);});}catch(const std::exception&e){_plugin_logprintf("[VRE] %s\n",e.what());return false;}}
bool commandExtract(int argc,char** argv) {try{if(argc<2)throw std::runtime_error("Usage: vre_extract \"new directory\"[, candidate index]");auto p=wide(argv[1]);auto i=argumentIndex(argc,argv,2);return submit([p,i]{extract(0,i,p);});}catch(const std::exception&e){_plugin_logprintf("[VRE] %s\n",e.what());return false;}}
bool commandDump(int argc,char** argv) {try{if(argc<2)throw std::runtime_error("Usage: vre_dump \"new directory\"[, module][, candidate index]");auto p=wide(argv[1]);auto m=argumentModule(argc,argv,2);auto i=argumentIndex(argc,argv,3);return submit([p,m,i]{scan(m);extract(m,i,p);});}catch(const std::exception&e){_plugin_logprintf("[VRE] %s\n",e.what());return false;}}
bool commandUI(int,char**) {if(HWND w=dispatcher.load()){PostMessageW(w,Show,0,0);if(!busy)submit([]{refreshModules();});return true;}return false;}
bool commandStatus(int,char**) {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);_plugin_logprintf("[VRE] busy=%u ok=%u candidates=%llu\n%s\n",busy?1u:0u,lastOk?1u:0u,static_cast<unsigned long long>(view.candidates.size()),narrow(view.status).c_str());return true;}
duint expression(int,const duint*,void* user) {
    switch(reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(user)) {
    case 0:return busy?1:0;
    case 1:{std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);return lastOk?1:0;}
    case 2:{std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);return view.candidates.size();}
    default:return 0;
    }
}
void callback(CBTYPE type,void* data) {
    if(stopping) return;
    if(type==CB_MENUENTRY){int id=static_cast<PLUG_CB_MENUENTRY*>(data)->hEntry;if(id==1)commandUI(0,nullptr);if(id==2)submit([]{scan(0);});if(id==3)commandStatus(0,nullptr);return;}
    if(type==CB_PAUSEDEBUG){++pauses;return;}
    if(type==CB_RESUMEDEBUG){invalidate(L"Target resumed. Scan again before extracting.");return;}
    if(type==CB_INITDEBUG||type==CB_STOPDEBUG||type==CB_STOPPINGDEBUG||type==CB_DETACH||type==CB_EXITPROCESS){invalidate(L"Load a program, run past unpacking, then scan.",true);return;}
    if(type==CB_LOADDLL||type==CB_UNLOADDLL){invalidate(L"Loaded modules changed. Refresh and scan again.");}
}
}

extern "C" __declspec(dllexport) bool pluginit(PLUG_INITSTRUCT* init) {
    pluginHandle=init->pluginHandle;init->sdkVersion=PLUG_SDKVERSION;init->pluginVersion=1;
    strcpy_s(init->pluginName,"VMP Resource Extractor");stopping=false;
    _plugin_registercommand(pluginHandle,"vre_ui",commandUI,false);
    _plugin_registercommand(pluginHandle,"vre_scan",commandScan,true);
    _plugin_registercommand(pluginHandle,"vre_extract",commandExtract,true);
    _plugin_registercommand(pluginHandle,"vre_dump",commandDump,true);
    _plugin_registercommand(pluginHandle,"vre_status",commandStatus,false);
    _plugin_registerexprfunction(pluginHandle,"vre_busy",0,expression,reinterpret_cast<void*>(0));
    _plugin_registerexprfunction(pluginHandle,"vre_ok",0,expression,reinterpret_cast<void*>(1));
    _plugin_registerexprfunction(pluginHandle,"vre_candidates",0,expression,reinterpret_cast<void*>(2));
    for(auto type:{CB_MENUENTRY,CB_INITDEBUG,CB_STOPPINGDEBUG,CB_STOPDEBUG,CB_DETACH,CB_EXITPROCESS,CB_RESUMEDEBUG,CB_PAUSEDEBUG,CB_LOADDLL,CB_UNLOADDLL})_plugin_registercallback(pluginHandle,type,callback);
    try{worker=std::thread(workerMain);uiThread=std::thread(uiMain);}catch(...){stopping=true;jobWake.notify_all();if(worker.joinable())worker.join();return false;}
    _plugin_logputs("[VRE] Loaded. Plugins > VMP Resource Extractor > Extract resources. Read-only memory capture; no driver or guest hooks.");return true;
}
extern "C" __declspec(dllexport) void plugsetup(PLUG_SETUPSTRUCT* setup) {
    _plugin_menuaddentry(setup->hMenu,1,"Extract resources...");
    _plugin_menuaddentry(setup->hMenu,2,"Scan main module");
    _plugin_menuaddseparator(setup->hMenu);
    _plugin_menuaddentry(setup->hMenu,3,"Status");
}
extern "C" __declspec(dllexport) bool plugstop() {
    stopping=true;++generation;jobWake.notify_all();
    if(HWND w=dispatcher.load())PostMessageW(w,Shutdown,0,0);
    if(worker.joinable())worker.join();
    if(uiThread.joinable())uiThread.join();
    for(auto name:{"vre_ui","vre_scan","vre_extract","vre_dump","vre_status"})_plugin_unregistercommand(pluginHandle,name);
    for(auto name:{"vre_busy","vre_ok","vre_candidates"})_plugin_unregisterexprfunction(pluginHandle,name);
    {std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(stateMutex);clearSnapshotLocked();}
    return true;
}
BOOL WINAPI DllMain(HINSTANCE h,DWORD reason,LPVOID) {if(reason==DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH)instance=h;return TRUE;}
